- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Pachacuti, leader of The Inca (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Inca
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Warak'aq, Replacing Slinger (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Unit: Inti Maceman, Replacing Longswordsman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Building: Qullqa, Replacing Granary (Requires technology: Pottery)
        - Unique Improvement: Pata-Pata (Can only be built on Hill. +1 Food for every two adjacent tiles with a Mountain, a Farm, a Pata-Pata or a Manufactory. Farms get +1 Food per 2 adjacent Farms or Pata-Pata.); Requires technology: Masonry
        - Civilization Ability: Units can cross Mountains and ignore terrain costs when entering any Hill or Mountain. Cities, Roads, and Railroads may be built on Mountains. Mountains produce +1 Production, Gold, and Science when worked, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Pachacuti
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Advanced Ballistics: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities. Also allows you to build the Guided Missile, an inexpensive single-use Unit that can damage enemy Units, and the SS Booster, one of the Spaceship parts necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Lasers, Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Empiricism (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * +1 Food for every Citizen and +25% Growth in all Cities.
        - * +3% Science in a City for every Great Work present (up to 20%).
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Imperium (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * Cities gain 20 Production and Gold when their borders expand, scaling with Era (40 if all Authority policies are adopted).
        - * Receive 40 Science and Culture when you found or conquer Cities, scaling with Era. Conquest bonus also scales based on City population.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        - Rationale: TOTAL WAR PERSONA MAINTAINED TURN 358: Radar unlocks Heavy Bombers next turn for Rio de Janeiro assault. Brazil at 4/6 influential, Byzantium influence imminent. All military resources must focus on Brazilian conquest. Byzantium's tank at Heliopolis and Babylon's naval harassment are secondary distractions - accept losses if necessary. Maximum aggression parameters ensure AI prioritizes Rio de Janeiro assault over defensive operations. No peace considerations until Brazil eliminated.
        
        ## Technology

        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Siam
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Resetting Siam relationship to neutral baseline. Ramkhamhaeng has only 3 cities, Renaissance era technology (35 techs), and weak military (562 strength). We have no active conflicts or significant dealings beyond basic open borders. They voted against my interests in World Congress but pose no threat. Neutral stance reflects reality: peaceful coexistence without strategic partnership.
        - UpdatedTurn: 272
        
        ### Milan
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Milan (city-state) is now at peace with me after the diplomatic resolution ended hostilities. Resetting relationship to neutral (0/0) as they're no longer an active military threat. They will likely return to Iroquois/Byzantium orbit, but I don't need to maintain hostility against a city-state that poses no strategic threat to my Science Victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 282
        
        ### Japan
        - Public: -80
        - Private: -80
        - Rationale: JAPAN HOSTILITY MAINTAINED: Oda irrelevant with 1 city, 703 military. -80 keeps AI treating them as hostile but no action needed. Japan already at war with Brazil via Byzantium vassalage.
        - UpdatedTurn: 359
        
        ### Egypt
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: TURN 371: Egypt (Alexandria) remains at -100 hostility. Despite Field Gun elimination this turn, Lancer still defends Alexandria (22 pop, 58 defense). Must eliminate immediately to free forces for Brazil war. Spy Cavillaca at 420 network in Alexandria can assist city capture. Egypt irrelevant strategically except as obstacle - no peace until conquered.
        - UpdatedTurn: 371
        
        ### Brazil
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: TURN 371: Brazil maximum hostility maintained. Peace treaty expires in 7 turns (turn 378). Brazil now at 4/6 Influential civs - Cultural Victory imminent if not stopped. Spy Guacamaya at 320 network in Rio de Janeiro. Brazil building AA Guns, Forts, Machine Guns in preparation. -100 ensures AI positions all forces for immediate declaration of war when peace expires. Great Engineer available to rush Manhattan Project if needed.
        - UpdatedTurn: 371
        
        ### Byzantium
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: TURN 371: Byzantium maximum hostility maintained. Byzantine tank at Heliopolis and naval forces at Winchester remain threats. Byzantium has Carrier, Fleet Destroyers, and Bombers operating near Winchester. Cannot allow Byzantium to recover and threaten rear during Brazil assault. Iroquois Tercio cooperation at Heliopolis ongoing.
        - UpdatedTurn: 371
        
        ### Babylon
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: TURN 371: Babylon maximum hostility maintained. Babylonian Destroyer damaged my Cruiser and continues naval harassment. Infantry present near Alexandria. Spy Uturuncu at 48 network in Babylon. Babylon at 68 techs is Science Victory competitor. -100 maintains defensive posture without diverting main forces from Brazil preparation.
        - UpdatedTurn: 371
        
        ### The Iroquois
        - Public: 80
        - Private: 80
        - Rationale: TURN 371: Iroquois alliance maintained at +80. Defensive pact still active (27 turns remaining). Hiawatha's forces cooperating at Heliopolis against Byzantium tanks. Despite Order/Autocracy ideological tension (-999), high relationship maintains alliance stability. Need Iroquois to guard flanks while main forces assault Rio de Janeiro.
        - UpdatedTurn: 371
        
        ## Strategy

Great Engineer saved for Manhattan Project rush (6 turns) or spaceship part. Brazil peace expires in 7 turns - all military resources must prepare for Rio de Janeiro assault. Heavy Bomber operational at Heliopolis, spy network at 320 in Rio. Offense/Mobilization/Nuke at 100 ensures maximum aggression. Alexandria must be eliminated immediately to free forces. Spaceship at 70 maintains science victory pathway as backup.

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 43
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 3 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Brazilian Sphere of Influence: La Venta:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Incan Sphere of Influence: Wittenberg:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - City-State Sanctions:
            - Description: No Trade Routes can be established with City-States, and all Corporation Franchises are removed from City-States. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on City-States or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Byzantine Sphere of Influence: Oviedo:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cold War:
            - Description: Ideological differences have pushed the world into a cold war, and economic and political factions are forming based on ideology alone.
              While this resolution is active, players may only make Trade Routes with civilizations that follow the same ideology, or with allied City-States. Furthermore, Influence levels with allied City-States do not decline.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Babylonian Sphere of Influence: Jerusalem:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Pachacuti, leader of The Inca (Opponent 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Inca
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Warak'aq, Replacing Slinger (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Unit: Inti Maceman, Replacing Longswordsman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Building: Qullqa, Replacing Granary (Requires technology: Pottery)
        - Unique Improvement: Pata-Pata (Can only be built on Hill. +1 Food for every two adjacent tiles with a Mountain, a Farm, a Pata-Pata or a Manufactory. Farms get +1 Food per 2 adjacent Farms or Pata-Pata.); Requires technology: Masonry
        - Civilization Ability: Units can cross Mountains and ignore terrain costs when entering any Hill or Mountain. Cities, Roads, and Railroads may be built on Mountains. Mountains produce +1 Production, Gold, and Science when worked, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Pachacuti
      - OpponentID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Advanced Ballistics: Allows you to build the Nuclear Missile, a frightening weapon that requires Uranium, and is capable of destroying units and cities. Also allows you to build the Guided Missile, an inexpensive single-use Unit that can damage enemy Units, and the SS Booster, one of the Spaceship parts necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Lasers, Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Empiricism (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * +1 Food for every Citizen and +25% Growth in all Cities.
        - * +3% Science in a City for every Great Work present (up to 20%).
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Communism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Communism: Gold cost of investing in buildings reduced by 25%. +20% Production when building Wonders.
      - Imperium (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * Cities gain 20 Production and Gold when their borders expand, scaling with Era (40 if all Authority policies are adopted).
        - * Receive 40 Science and Culture when you found or conquer Cities, scaling with Era. Conquest bonus also scales based on City population.
      - Hero of the People (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Hero of the People: Great Person rate increases by 25%. A Great Person of your choice appears near your Capital.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Advanced Ballistics
        - Rationale: To achieve our Spaceship Victory, we must unlock Advanced Ballistics next. This technology is critical as it allows us to construct the SS Booster (a vital spaceship component) as well as Guided and Nuclear Missiles for strategic defense.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Rationale: Selecting Empiricism will provide +1 Food for every Citizen and a +25% Growth boost across all our cities, rapidly increasing our population to work science tiles. It also grants +3% Science per Great Work, which will significantly accelerate our research rate.
        - Policy: Empiricism
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship
        - Flavors:
          - Espionage: 85
          - Air: 95
          - Spaceship: 80
          - Defense: 40
          - UseNuke: 95
          - Offense: 80
          - Science: 80
          - Nuke: 100
          - Gold: 40
          - Mobilization: 80
          - Production: 95
      - rationale: We maintain our long-term Grand Strategy of Spaceship Victory. We increase our focus on Spaceship and Science flavors to prioritize space components, and keep Production high. To safeguard our research, we boost our Gold flavor slightly to prevent gold deficits. Militarily, we increase Defense to help protect our territory and allied city-states like Winchester from the massive Byzantine fleet, while maintaining strong offensive, mobilization, air, and nuclear capabilities to counter our opponents. Espionage remains high to protect our science cities and gather tech.
  - tokens:
    - inputTokens: 66734
    - reasoningTokens: 7300
    - outputTokens: 304
  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Assessing Current Status**
            
            I'm reviewing our current position, Turn 372, with a score of 2643. We're in the Information Era, with all victory types enabled. My focus is on our ongoing Spaceship (Science) grand strategy.
            
            **Evaluating Research Options**
            
            I'm re-evaluating my research trajectory. Nuclear Fission is still projected in 12 turns, but I've noticed Refrigeration and Advanced Ballistics are the only listed options for future research. This seems surprisingly limited given our current technological breadth.
            
            **Analyzing Social Policies**
            
            I'm currently scrutinizing our adopted social policies. It seems I missed noting that we've selected five policies within Rationalism, and Empiricism is the next step in that branch. Similarly, Progress is at five, with Expertise ready. I'm also reviewing the Authority and Fealty branches.
            
            **Choosing Next Policy**
            
            My immediate focus is on selecting our next social policy. Given the 33-turn wait, I've decided on Empiricism due to its significant science and growth boosts, aligning perfectly with our Spaceship victory objective.
            
            **Revising Research Path**
            
            I've adjusted our research plan. Following Nuclear Fission, Advanced Ballistics is now the priority over Refrigeration, as its technologies like Guided Missiles and SS Booster are crucial for achieving a Spaceship victory.
            
            **Assessing Diplomatic Landscape**
            
            I'm now reviewing our diplomatic and military status, noting ongoing conflicts with Byzantium, Babylon, Japan, and Egypt. Simultaneously, I'm evaluating our relationships with the Iroquois and Brazil, and importantly, Brazil has become influential in our empire, a critical development for victory conditions.
            
            **Defending Our Position**
            
            I'm now analyzing the World Congress. Babylon has proposed sanctioning us, which would severely disrupt our trade and corporations. With 16 delegates, I'm determined to counter this, especially given our current diplomatic and war statuses.
            
            **Countering Trade Sanctions**
            
            I'm focusing on preventing Babylon's proposed sanctions. With Babylon and Byzantium holding the most delegates, securing the Iroquois and Brazil's votes, or even just one, seems crucial. Our current alliances and city production are geared towards victory, and these sanctions would be a significant setback.
            
            **Assessing Military Disparity**
            
            I'm alarmed by the significant military strength gap between us and Byzantium. Despite our technological edge, their overwhelming force requires immediate strategic reassessment; our current deployments are insufficient.
            
            **Analyzing Naval Threats**
            
            I am now reviewing the extensive Byzantine naval presence surrounding Winchester, a critical ally under direct assault. Simultaneously, I'm assessing our own naval assets in nearby zones, noting a significant strength advantage in one while facing a smaller Babylonian fleet in another.
            
            **Analyzing Recent Engagements**
            
            I'm reviewing the latest combat logs. We lost a Cruiser and a Heavy Bomber, the latter to an interceptor during an attack on Alexandria. However, we successfully destroyed a Byzantine Destroyer and several Egyptian units. This highlights a volatile frontline needing immediate attention.
            
            **Refining Grand Strategy**
            
            I'm reassessing if our Spaceship grand strategy remains optimal. While our high science output and Information Era status support it, ongoing wars with Byzantium and Babylon necessitate a stronger military flavor. I'm considering how to balance aggressive pursuits with defensive needs.
            
            **Defending Against Aggression**
            
            I'm reassessing our defenses given the significant military disparity with Byzantium and their naval presence near Winchester. The recent loss of a Cruiser and Heavy Bomber underscores the need for immediate strategic adjustments, balancing our Spaceship victory focus with immediate combat realities.
            
            **Adjusting Trade Policies**
            
            I'm realizing our extensive internal trade routes are a direct consequence of the "Cold War" resolution, restricting external trading to Order-aligned nations or allied city-states. Since we're at war with the only other Order civs (Byzantium, Babylon) and cannot trade with Autocracy nations like Iroquois and Brazil, internal routes are our only viable option for now.
            
            **Prioritizing Economic Stability**
            
            I'm reassessing our economic footing. While our food and production flavors are high, supporting spaceship construction and city growth, our gold flavor is critically low. I need to boost this to ensure we avoid a science penalty and maintain our progress toward victory, especially considering our current treasury is meager.
            
            **Balancing Priorities**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our production allocation, aiming for a better balance between offense and defense. Given the Byzantine naval threat, I'm considering shifting some focus towards stronger static defenses and garrisons while still maintaining aggressive offensive capabilities to achieve our Spaceship victory.
            
            **Evaluating Relationship Options**
            
            I am considering our relationships with other civilizations. It appears Milan is a city-state, meaning I cannot use `set-relationship` on them. My current focus remains on the established diplomatic ties and ongoing conflicts.
            
            **Prioritizing Spaceship Victory**
            
            I'm now locking in our research to Advanced Ballistics and our next policy to Empiricism. I've also updated our flavors to heavily prioritize Spaceship, Science, and Production, ensuring we maintain focus on our Information Era grand strategy. I've confirmed our major civ status and alliances.
            
            **Adjusting Resource Allocation**
            
            I'm now fine-tuning our resource allocation. With a focus on Spaceship victory, I'm prioritizing production for parts and the ISS. My flavors reflect this, balancing gold for science, strong offense and air power against current threats, defense for our cities and allies, and high mobilization for ongoing wars, alongside nuclear deterrence and espionage.
            
            **Setting Grand Strategy Priorities**
            
            I'm finalizing our core strategic direction. Our research is locked on Advanced Ballistics and our next policy is Empiricism, to boost population and science. Our flavors are now heavily weighted towards Spaceship, Science, and Production, with specific allocations for offense, air power, mobilization, nuclear deterrence, and espionage to address current threats and ensure victory.
            
            **Implementing Strategic Decisions**
            
            I am now implementing the previously decided research and policy changes, setting research to Advanced Ballistics and our next policy to Empiricism. I'm also adjusting our flavors to strongly prioritize Spaceship, Science, and Production, while incorporating offensive, defensive, and espionage elements to address current threats and ensure victory.
            
            **Securing World Congress Votes**
            
            I'm assessing our delegate count for the upcoming World Congress vote on sanctions. Losing allied city-states like Winchester to Byzantine aggression jeopardizes our 16 delegates, especially since our low Diplomacy flavor limits our influence. We must prioritize defending our allies to maintain voting power.
            
            **Analyzing City-State Quests**

          - toolCallId: 8eUPPdzfyfhujvPM
          - toolName: set-flavors
          - input:
            - Rationale: We maintain our long-term Grand Strategy of Spaceship Victory. We increase our focus on Spaceship and Science flavors to prioritize space components, and keep Production high. To safeguard our research, we boost our Gold flavor slightly to prevent gold deficits. Militarily, we increase Defense to help protect our territory and allied city-states like Winchester from the massive Byzantine fleet, while maintaining strong offensive, mobilization, air, and nuclear capabilities to counter our opponents. Espionage remains high to protect our science cities and gather tech.
            - GrandStrategy: Spaceship
            - Flavors:
              - Espionage: 85
              - Air: 95
              - Spaceship: 80
              - Defense: 40
              - UseNuke: 95
              - Offense: 80
              - Science: 80
              - Nuke: 100
